# Issues

> Generated by `node tools/scripts/issues/build-issues.mjs` from `tools/scripts/issues/issues.src.txt`. Edit the source file, not this one. Upload instructions: [issues_upload_instructions.md](issues_upload_instructions.md).

74 issues across 8 milestones. Rules for working through them: [workflow.md](workflow.md). Each issue below has a copy-pastable AI prompt; the JSON array at the end is the bulk-import payload for `gh`.

## How progress is tracked

- **GitHub Issues are the live tracker.** Open/closed state, the `status:*` labels (`proposed`, `in-progress`, `in-review`, `blocked`), the milestone and the issue comments replace the old status column and progress log.
- This file is the **definition** of the backlog. When scope changes, edit `issues.src.txt`, regenerate this file, and update the affected GitHub issue.
- Upload order is a verified topological order: every dependency appears before its dependents.

## Audit summary

1. Re-tagged every issue by repo area (T, R, P, L, H, D, U, Q) and numbered each tag from 01 in dependency order; the previous GTP-0NN IDs are kept as `legacy`.
2. Split issues that mixed backend and UI so each issue touches one area: GTP-055 into L-08 (recipe save) and U-06 (apply-settings UI); GTP-056 into P-12 (export job) and U-11 (export dialog); GTP-064 into U-08 (scanner UI) and U-12 (dust panel); GTP-072 into H-06 (triplet import job) and U-09 (triplet UI).
3. Added L-09: thumbnails are persisted and must be regenerated after a recipe changes (spec 5.1); the old list never did this.
4. Added L-10: one shared service that estimates the roll film base from the first ingested frame; import, scan and capture all need it (spec 4.2).
5. Corrected dependencies: JobManager lives in gtp-app so L-01 depends on P-10; scan, triplet import and capture jobs depend on the import commit path (L-05) and L-10; the capture orchestrator depends on the triplet import path (H-06); U-12 depends on the IR stage (P-13).
6. Moved cross-cutting requirements into acceptance criteria: `GTP_FAKE_HW=1` selects fixture/fake devices (H-01, D-04), the light is switched off on app exit and view unmount (D-04, U-10), the update check can be disabled (T-06), the macOS-only `tether` feature is off for Windows builds (T-05, H-09).
7. Every issue now has one area, one type, one size, an estimate, labels, explicit dependencies and a self-contained AI prompt; the upload order is a verified topological order, so every dependency is created before its dependents.
8. Checked consistency: no dependency points to a later milestone; every crate and top-level folder in structure.md and architecture.md is owned by at least one issue; each issue has one primary area (a thin src-tauri command may accompany a backend service issue).

## Tags, labels, milestones

| Tag | Area | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| `T-NN` | Tooling, CI and release | .github/, tools/, root configs |
| `R-NN` | Research spikes | docs/research/, decisions/ |
| `P-NN` | Image pipeline | crates/gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-app (pipeline, develop, export) |
| `L-NN` | Library and data | crates/gtp-catalog, gtp-app services (library, import, tags, search, jobs) |
| `H-NN` | Hardware ingest and capture | crates/gtp-ingest, crates/gtp-capture |
| `D-NN` | Desktop shell (Tauri) | apps/desktop/src-tauri |
| `U-NN` | User interface | apps/desktop/src, packages/ui |
| `Q-NN` | Quality, docs and release verification | tools/benchmark, docs/, samples/ |

Labels: `area:*` (one per issue), `type:*`, `size:S|M` (S = 0.5d, M = 1d), `needs:hw|data|mac|human` (the assistant prepares steps; the human acts and reports), `adr`, and `status:*`.

| Milestone | Exit criteria |
|---|---|
| M0 Foundations | App launches on Windows x64 and macOS; hardware spikes answered |
| M1 Pipeline on files | Quality metric calibrated; budgets met on fixtures |
| M2 Library | Create library, import 36 frames, tag, search under budget |
| M3 Develop UI | Full file-based workflow usable end to end |
| M4 LS-50 ingest | Full-roll scan with IR on each OS |
| M5 RGB triplets (files) | D600 NEF triplet converts correctly |
| M6 Guided RGB capture | Roll captured with Scanlight + D600 on macOS, then Windows |
| M7 MVP release v0.1.0 | Budgets and quality metric met; release checklist passed |

## Issues

### T-01 – Initialize Git repository and LFS

- Milestone: M0 Foundations | Estimate: 0.5d (size S) | Type: chore
- Labels: area:tooling, type:chore, size:S, needs:human
- Depends on: none
- Previous ID: GTP-001 | Spec: structure.md section 6; convention section 9

**Description**

Put the scaffold under version control on branch main and prove that the ignore and Git LFS rules in .gitignore and .gitattributes work.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Git and Git LFS are installed (human) and `git --version`, `git lfs version` work
- [ ] Repository on `main` with one commit containing the scaffold; `git status` is clean
- [ ] `git check-ignore samples/private/x.tif` and `git check-ignore target/` report both ignored
- [ ] `git lfs track` lists the TIFF, TIFF, DNG, NEF and ICC patterns (NEF added if missing)

<details><summary>Copy-paste AI prompt</summary>

```text
You are working on the project "Grain to Pixel" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.

PROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)
- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).
- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.
- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.

TECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)
- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.
- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.
- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.
- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.
- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.

ISSUE T-01 – Initialize Git repository and LFS
Milestone: M0 Foundations | Area: Tooling, CI and release | Type: chore | Size: S (0.5d)
Spec references: structure.md section 6; convention section 9
Dependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): none

DESCRIPTION
Put the scaffold under version control on branch main and prove that the ignore and Git LFS rules in .gitignore and .gitattributes work.

TECHNICAL DETAILS
FILES: .gitattributes (add `*.nef filter=lfs diff=lfs merge=lfs -text` if missing).
COMMANDS: `git init -b main`, `git lfs install`, `git add .`, `git commit -m "chore: scaffold monorepo"`.
ROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.
TESTS: paste the outputs of the check-ignore and lfs track commands in the report.
OUT OF SCOPE: creating the GitHub remote or pushing (the human does that).

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
- Git and Git LFS are installed (human) and `git --version`, `git lfs version` work
- Repository on `main` with one commit containing the scaffold; `git status` is clean
- `git check-ignore samples/private/x.tif` and `git check-ignore target/` report both ignored
- `git lfs track` lists the TIFF, TIFF, DNG, NEF and ICC patterns (NEF added if missing)

Needs from the human: an action only the human can do. Prepare exact steps and a checklist; never tick these items yourself.
Definition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.
```

### T-02 – Lint, format and dependency-rule tooling

- Milestone: M0 Foundations | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: chore
- Labels: area:tooling, type:chore, size:M
- Depends on: T-01
- Previous ID: GTP-002 | Spec: convention sections 1, 3, 8; architecture section 3.3

**Description**

Make the convention rules enforceable locally: pinned toolchain, workspace lints, formatters, licence policy, ESLint/Prettier/tsconfig, and a script that enforces the allowed crate dependency edges.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] rust-toolchain.toml pins stable; `[workspace.lints]` denies unwrap_used, expect_used, panic, todo, print_stdout, print_stderr, dbg_macro and forbids unsafe_code; every crate inherits them
- [ ] `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo deny check` pass
- [ ] ESLint (typescript-eslint, jsx-a11y, no-console, no literal strings in JSX), Prettier and a strict tsconfig base exist; `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test` run
- [ ] `tools/scripts/check-deps` enforces the allowed-edges table of architecture.md section 3.3, passes on the workspace and fails on a bad fixture

<details><summary>Copy-paste AI prompt</summary>

```text
You are working on the project "Grain to Pixel" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.

PROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)
- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).
- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.
- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.

TECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)
- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.
- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.
- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.

ISSUE T-02 – Lint, format and dependency-rule tooling
Milestone: M0 Foundations | Area: Tooling, CI and release | Type: chore | Size: M (1d)
Spec references: convention sections 1, 3, 8; architecture section 3.3
Dependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): T-01 – Initialize Git repository and LFS

DESCRIPTION
Make the convention rules enforceable locally: pinned toolchain, workspace lints, formatters, licence policy, ESLint/Prettier/tsconfig, and a script that enforces the allowed crate dependency edges.

TECHNICAL DETAILS
FILES: rust-toolchain.toml, rustfmt.toml, deny.toml (licence allowlist), .nvmrc (20), eslint.config.js, .prettierrc, tsconfig.base.json; root Cargo.toml `[workspace.lints.rust] unsafe_code = "forbid"` and `[workspace.lints.clippy]` deny list; every crates/*/Cargo.toml gets `[lints] workspace = true`; package.json scripts lint, typecheck, test (`pnpm -r --if-present`), format; tools/scripts/check-deps/ (small Rust bin using `cargo metadata`) with tests/fixtures/bad-workspace (gtp-edit depends on gtp-negative); .gitattributes unchanged.
INTERFACES: `check-deps [--manifest-path <Cargo.toml>]` exits 1 and prints each forbidden edge. Allowed edges: core -> none; io, negative, edit, catalog, capture -> core; ingest -> core, io; app -> all gtp crates; src-tauri -> app, core; tools/benchmark -> app, core.
ROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.
TESTS: integration test: check-deps passes on this workspace, fails on the bad fixture; a deliberate `unwrap()` fails clippy (show, then remove).
PREREQUISITES (human): pnpm installed.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
- rust-toolchain.toml pins stable; `[workspace.lints]` denies unwrap_used, expect_used, panic, todo, print_stdout, print_stderr, dbg_macro and forbids unsafe_code; every crate inherits them
- `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo deny check` pass
- ESLint (typescript-eslint, jsx-a11y, no-console, no literal strings in JSX), Prettier and a strict tsconfig base exist; `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test` run
- `tools/scripts/check-deps` enforces the allowed-edges table of architecture.md section 3.3, passes on the workspace and fails on a bad fixture

Needs from the human: approval of your proposal.
Definition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.
```

### D-01 – Tauri 2 desktop skeleton

- Milestone: M0 Foundations | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: feature
- Labels: area:desktop-shell, type:feature, size:M, needs:mac
- Depends on: T-02
- Previous ID: GTP-003 | Spec: specification sections 2, 3, 9; architecture sections 3.1, 4

**Description**

Scaffold apps/desktop (React + TypeScript + Vite UI, Tauri 2 shell) so a window launches on Windows and macOS with the project layout, strict CSP and minimal capabilities.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] `pnpm dev` opens the window on Windows; the macOS launch is confirmed by the human
- [ ] CSP is `default-src 'self'`, no shell plugin, capabilities list only what the stub needs
- [ ] src-tauri is a Cargo workspace member and passes the workspace lints and check-deps
- [ ] A UI test renders the app shell and switches between Library, Develop and Import views

<details><summary>Copy-paste AI prompt</summary>

```text
You are working on the project "Grain to Pixel" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.

PROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)
- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).
- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.
- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.

TECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)
- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.
- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.
- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.
- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.
- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.

ISSUE D-01 – Tauri 2 desktop skeleton
Milestone: M0 Foundations | Area: Desktop shell (Tauri) | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)

### R-01 – Spike - nkscan with the modded LS-50

- Milestone: M0 Foundations | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: spike
- Labels: area:research, type:spike, size:M, needs:hw, adr
- Depends on: T-01
- Previous ID: GTP-008 | Spec: specification section 6; ADR 0003; docs/research/nkscan.md

**Description**

Decide whether nkscan can drive the whole-roll LS-50 (SA-30 roll adapter via coolscan-mods firmware): detection, 16-bit RGB + IR output, advance across 3+ frames, and the Windows driver stack.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Device detected and identified as an SA-30 roll adapter on Windows and macOS (human reports)
- [ ] 16-bit RGB and a separate IR plane obtained; format and size recorded
- [ ] Advance across 3 consecutive frames works, or the failure is characterized
- [ ] Windows driver outcome recorded (VueScan driver vs WinUSB/Zadig)
- [ ] docs/research/nkscan.md checklist filled; ADR 0003 set to Accepted, amended or Rejected with a recommendation

<details><summary>Copy-paste AI prompt</summary>

```text
You are working on the project "Grain to Pixel" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.

PROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)
- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).
- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.
- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.

TECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)
- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.
- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.
- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.
- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.
- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.

### R-02 – Spike - Scanlight V4 serial control

- Milestone: M0 Foundations | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: spike
- Labels: area:research, type:spike, size:M, needs:hw, adr
- Depends on: T-01
- Previous ID: GTP-009 | Spec: specification section 6.1; ADR 0004; docs/research/scanlight-capture.md

**Description**

Confirm the Scanlight V4 binary serial protocol on the real device (rev B), measure timing and temperature, and capture packet recordings to use as test vectors.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Serial port opens on macOS and Windows without extra drivers; firmware ID recorded (rev B expected)
- [ ] Each colour lights alone via SET_COLOR with save flag 0; switch latency and needed settle time recorded
- [ ] LED_TEMP over a 3-colour sequence recorded and warn/abort limits justified
- [ ] Pulse length that reliably fires the D600 recorded, with or without SET_FOCUS, using the owned remote cable
- [ ] Captured packets saved under samples/public/scanlight/ and the research note updated

ISSUE R-02 – Spike - Scanlight V4 serial control
Milestone: M0 Foundations | Area: Research spikes | Type: spike | Size: M (1d)
Spec references: specification section 6.1; ADR 0004; docs/research/scanlight-capture.md
Dependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): T-01 – Initialize Git repository and LFS

DESCRIPTION
Confirm the Scanlight V4 binary serial protocol on the real device (rev B), measure timing and temperature, and capture packet recordings to use as test vectors.

TECHNICAL DETAILS
SCOPE: packet format `0xFE`, header, length, data. Host commands: SET_COLOR (header 0; R, G, B, W, IR, save flag; save always 0), GET_FW_VERSION (2), SHUTTER_PULSE (3; 10 ms units), SET_FOCUS (7; PCB 26a902b). Device packets: LED_TEMP (1, int32 millidegrees C), VBUS (2, mV), FW_VERSION (3). No acks, no VID/PID: probe ports with GET_FW_VERSION. Throwaway script (python/pyserial or Rust) outside crates/.
OUTPUT: docs/research/scanlight-capture.md, ADR 0004 amendments, recorded packets as test vectors, follow-up issues. The light must be off at the end of every run.
PROCESS: assistant prepares commands; human runs; assistant records; hardware items never ticked by the assistant.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
- Serial port opens on macOS and Windows without extra drivers; firmware ID recorded (rev B expected)
- Each colour lights alone via SET_COLOR with save flag 0; switch latency and needed settle time recorded
- LED_TEMP over a 3-colour sequence recorded and warn/abort limits justified
- Pulse length that reliably fires the D600 recorded, with or without SET_FOCUS, using the owned remote cable
- Captured packets saved under samples/public/scanlight/ and the research note updated

Needs from the human: real hardware run and report. Prepare exact steps and a checklist; never tick these items yourself.
Definition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.
```

</details>

- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.
- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.

TECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)
- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.
- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.
- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.
- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.
- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.

ISSUE P-01 – Core domain types and Recipe v1
Milestone: M1 Pipeline on files | Area: Image pipeline | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)
Spec references: specification sections 4, 4.1; architecture section 3.4
Dependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): T-02 – Lint, format and dependency-rule tooling

DESCRIPTION
The shared domain model and a versioned, validated Recipe in gtp-core, with no I/O.

TECHNICAL DETAILS
FILES (crates/gtp-core): src/{lib.rs, ids.rs, image.rs}; src/model/{roll,frame,source,tag}.rs; src/recipe/{mod,v1,clamp,migrate}.rs.
TYPES: ULID newtypes RollId, FrameId, TagId; `ImageF32 { width, height, channels, data: Vec<f32> }` (linear, row-major); Roll, Frame, FrameKind {ScanRgbIr, RgbTriplet}, SourceFile, SourceRole {Rgb, Ir, R, G, B}, Tag; Recipe v1 per spec 4.1 (geometry, base, invert, color, tone with curve points, dust, output); `Recipe::clamp()`; `migrate(json: &str) -> Result<Recipe>`.
ROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.
TESTS: JSON round trip, unknown/missing fields, clamp idempotence and ranges (proptest), migrate rejects v2, ID parse failures.
OUT OF SCOPE: TIFF I/O, pipeline stages.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
- Recipe round-trips through JSON; unknown fields ignored; missing fields default
- Clamping is idempotent (property test) and covers every documented range
- `migrate` handles v1 and rejects unknown future versions with UnsupportedFormat
- ID types parse and reject malformed input

Needs from the human: approval of your proposal.
Definition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.
```

</details>

### P-02 – Synthetic negative test fixtures

### P-03 – TIFF read/write with input limits

- Milestone: M1 Pipeline on files | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: feature
- Labels: area:pipeline, type:feature, size:M
- Depends on: P-01
- Previous ID: GTP-022 | Spec: specification sections 5.1 and 9; convention section 4

**Description**

Safe 16-bit TIFF reading and writing in gtp-io for scans, IR planes and library originals, with size limits checked before allocation.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] A crafted header with huge dimensions is rejected without a large allocation
- [ ] Corrupt and truncated files return typed errors and never panic
- [ ] Deflate + predictor output is smaller than raw on the synthetic image

### P-07 – Frame-area detection

- Milestone: M1 Pipeline on files | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: feature
- Labels: area:pipeline, type:feature, size:M
- Depends on: P-05
- Previous ID: GTP-026 | Spec: specification section 4.2 (frame detection)

**Description**

Detect the exposed frame rectangle from the base-normalized luminance with a confidence value.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] On synthetic frames the rectangle matches ground truth within 0.5 percent of each side
- [ ] Blank or noisy input returns low confidence and the full frame
- [ ] Works with rebate on four sides and on two sides

<details><summary>Copy-paste AI prompt</summary>

```text
You are working on the project "Grain to Pixel" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.

PROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)
- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).
- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.
- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.

TECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)
- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.
- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.
- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.
- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.
- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.

ROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.
TESTS: ground-truth rectangles, low-confidence fallback, partial rebate.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
- On synthetic frames the rectangle matches ground truth within 0.5 percent of each side
- Blank or noisy input returns low confidence and the full frame
- Works with rebate on four sides and on two sides

- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.

### L-02 – Catalog schema, migrations, backup and integrity

- Milestone: M2 Library | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: feature

ISSUE L-02 – Catalog schema, migrations, backup and integrity
Milestone: M2 Library | Area: Library and data | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)
Spec references: specification sections 5.2 and 5.3; architecture section 4.1

The SQLite catalog crate with repositories, forward-only migrations, pre-migration backup and integrity check.

FILES: crates/gtp-catalog/{Cargo.toml, src/{lib.rs, db.rs, backup.rs, fts.rs}, src/repo/{roll,frame,source,recipe,tag,search,meta}.rs, migrations/{0001_init.sql, 0002_search.sql, 0003_export_log.sql}}.

Pragmas: WAL, foreign_keys=ON, synchronous=NORMAL. rusqlite (bundled) + rusqlite_migration.
INTERFACES: `Catalog::open(path) -> Result<Catalog>`, `integrity_check()`, repositories per entity.
ROUTES: none.
TESTS: migrate from empty, idempotent reopen, backup created, cascade, NOCASE uniqueness, LibraryTooNew, FTS rows maintained in the same transaction.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
- Migrations apply on an empty database on both OS and reopening is idempotent
- A backup `library.db.bak-v<N>` is created before migrating
- Cascade deletes work; tag names are unique case-insensitively (Unicode, via a `name_key` column)
- A newer schema returns LibraryTooNew
- Rolls carry camera, box and shot ISO and a partial-date shooting range; roll tags (`roll_tag`) count for every frame in search

TECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)
- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.
- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.
- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.
- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.
- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.

ISSUE L-04 – Roll and frame commands
Milestone: M2 Library | Area: Library and data | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)
Spec references: specification sections 4, 5.3, 6
Dependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): L-03 – Library create/open, folder layout and settings

DESCRIPTION
CRUD for rolls and frames over the catalog through gtp-app services and thin Tauri commands.

TECHNICAL DETAILS
FILES: crates/gtp-app/src/services/roll_frame.rs; apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/{roll.rs, frame.rs}.
ROUTES: `roll_create(RollInput) -> Roll`, `roll_update(RollId, RollPatch) -> Roll`, `roll_list() -> Roll[]`, `roll_delete(RollId)`, `roll_set_film_base(RollId, [f32;3]) -> Roll`, `frame_list(FrameQuery { roll_id?, page, page_size }) -> Page<FrameSummary>`, `frame_get(FrameId) -> Frame`, `frame_delete(FrameId[])`, `frame_set_note(FrameId, String)`.
MIGRATIONS: none.
TESTS: services against a temp library, injected transaction failure keeps files, command-layer error mapping.

Milestone: M2 Library | Area: Library and data | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)

FLOW per frame: write originals (Deflate+predictor), sha256 verify, mark read-only, 512 px JPEG thumbnail, one catalog transaction (frame, source_file rows, default recipe, search text), emit `library://changed`.

TESTS: end-to-end import, cancel, corrupt/oversize, timing.

### L-10 – Roll film base estimated on first ingested frame

- Milestone: M2 Library | Estimate: 0.5d (size S) | Type: feature
- Labels: area:library, type:feature, size:S
- Depends on: P-05, L-04, L-05

Audit gap. After the first frame of a roll is ingested (import, scan or capture), auto-estimate the film base and store it on the roll so later frames share it; a frame may override it.

TESTS: first-frame set, second-frame no overwrite, manual base preserved, low-confidence estimate stored with a warning flag.

- The first ingested frame sets the roll film base when none exists; later frames never overwrite it silently
- The service exposes one function used by the import, scan and capture services
- A roll with manual base is left untouched

### L-06 – Tags and full-text search

- Milestone: M2 Library | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: feature

- Depends on: L-04
- Previous ID: GTP-045 | Spec: specification sections 5.2, 6, 11

**Description**

Tag management and tag/full-text search over the FTS5 table, kept consistent in the same transaction as parent changes.

ISSUE P-11 – Develop service - open, analyze and render
Milestone: M3 Develop UI | Area: Image pipeline | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)
Spec references: specification sections 3, 6, 11; architecture section 3.5
Dependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): P-10 – Application layer gtp-app - pipeline, proxy and analyze; L-04 – Roll and frame commands; L-05 – TIFF import job with hashing and thumbnails

DESCRIPTION
Backend for interactive editing: load a frame, build and cache the proxy, analyze, and render previews over binary IPC with stale-request dropping.

TECHNICAL DETAILS
FILES: crates/gtp-app/src/services/develop.rs; apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/develop.rs.
ROUTES: `develop_open(FrameId) -> DevelopInfo { width, height, has_ir, recipe, roll_base }`, `develop_analyze(FrameId) -> Analysis`, `develop_render(FrameId, Recipe, max_edge: u32, seq: u64) -> binary` (16-byte header: seq u64, width u32, height u32; then RGBA8) via `tauri::ipc::Response`.
VALIDATION: max_edge in [256, 4096].
MIGRATIONS: none.
TESTS: open/render on synthetic frames, header layout, LRU eviction at the 5th frame, superseded render, clamping, timing.
DELIVERY (decided when P-11 was approved): a superseded render is dropped by checking its sequence number before it starts (after waiting for the frame's render turn) and again before it returns, so no change to the pipeline stages; cooperative cancellation inside the stages is left for later if the budget needs it. The analysis type is named `DevelopAnalysis` on the IPC boundary. The infrared plane is not loaded yet (`has_ir` is reported; dust removal, P-13, will load it). `develop_render` is a raw-bytes command, routed through `ipc::RAW_COMMANDS` like `frame_thumbnail`; `shared/ipc.ts` gets `developOpen`, `developAnalyze` and `developRender` (with the header parsed), without any UI.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
- `develop_open` loads the original and caches a 2048 px proxy in an LRU of 4
- `develop_render` returns RGBA8 sRGB bytes with a header and drops superseded requests as Cancelled
- 1600 px render time recorded against the 100 ms budget; recipe clamped server-side
- No pixel data in logs; ipc-types regenerated

ISSUE L-08 – Recipe save and apply to frames
Milestone: M3 Develop UI | Area: Library and data | Type: feature | Size: S (0.5d)
Spec references: specification sections 4.1 and 6
Dependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): P-11 – Develop service - open, analyze and render

DESCRIPTION
Persist recipes and copy selected recipe fields to other frames or a whole roll.

TECHNICAL DETAILS
FILES: crates/gtp-app/src/services/develop.rs (`save_recipe`, `apply_to`); src-tauri/src/commands/develop.rs.
ROUTES: `recipe_save(FrameId, Recipe)`, `recipe_apply_to(FrameId[], source: FrameId, fields: RecipeField[])`; `enum RecipeField { Geometry, Base, Invert, Color, Tone, Dust, Output }`.
MIGRATIONS: none (recipe table exists).
TESTS: round trip, selected-field copy, clamping.
DELIVERY (decided when L-08 was approved): a target frame that was never edited starts from the default recipe, and the chosen fields are copied over it (its own auto recipe is not computed). The `Base` field copies the base source together with its value. Also: `Color` copies the colour balance and the black-and-white conversion (`mono`), which share a panel; the pure copy (`RecipeField`, `Recipe::copy_fields_from`) lives in gtp-core so it is unit-tested without a library; the catalog gets `save_recipes`, which stores several recipes in one transaction so applying to a roll is all or nothing; a recipe whose tone curve has a single point is refused, since the pipeline could not render it; `recipe_save` returns the recipe as stored.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
- `recipe_save` writes the recipe row and edited_at; reload returns the same recipe
- `recipe_apply_to` copies only the selected fields
- Invalid recipes are clamped before storing

ISSUE L-09 – Regenerate thumbnail when a recipe changes
Milestone: M3 Develop UI | Area: Library and data | Type: feature | Size: S (0.5d)
Spec references: specification section 5.1 (thumbnails are a rebuildable cache)
Dependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): L-08 – Recipe save and apply to frames; L-05 – TIFF import job with hashing and thumbnails

DESCRIPTION
Audit gap. Thumbnails must reflect the saved recipe; regenerate the 512 px thumbnail in the background after recipe_save and for applied frames.

TECHNICAL DETAILS
FILES: crates/gtp-app/src/services/develop.rs (thumbnail task using `pipeline::render` Preview at 512 px and `gtp-io` JPEG encode).
ROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.
TESTS: thumbnail changes after recipe change (pixel difference), debounce, rebuild-if-missing.
DELIVERY (decided when L-09 was approved): the grid learns which pictures changed from an optional list of frame ids on the `library://changed` payload (`frame_ids`, empty for other changes), so only those cached images are dropped and loaded again, with the change event sent when a batch is done (the shared `LibraryEvents` trait gets `frames_changed`). A missing thumbnail is rebuilt inside `frame_thumbnail` while the grid waits, one rebuild at a time. The service is `services/thumbnails.rs` (not `develop.rs`), with one worker thread and a 500 ms quiet period; a thumbnail is rendered from the cached proxy when the frame is open, otherwise from the original decoded without caching it, so a batch cannot evict the frame being edited. A failure keeps the old thumbnail and is logged (gtp-app gains the `tracing` dependency); a read-only library is never written to.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
- After recipe_save the thumbnail file is replaced and `library://changed` is emitted
- Regeneration is debounced and never blocks recipe_save
- A missing thumbnail is rebuilt on demand

Needs from the human: approval of your proposal.
Definition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.
```

Milestone: M3 Develop UI | Area: User interface | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)

The Develop view shell: canvas fed by binary IPC with pan/zoom, filmstrip, histogram and a before/after toggle.

Zoom runs from fit to 400% of the 2048 px proxy (past 100% pixels are enlarged, not smoothed); true 1:1 inspection at full resolution would need a full-resolution tile render, which is not in the plan. A frame opens with a double-click or Enter in the Library grid, and the filmstrip lists the frames of the Library's current view (the same query as the grid, shared through `use-library-query`). The histogram is computed in the browser from the preview's pixels, so no backend change was needed. `Histogram` is drawn as SVG in `packages/ui`. Keys beyond the two in the criteria: Escape (back to the Library) and 0, 1, + and - (zoom), all in one map (`develop-keys.ts`) for the customizable shortcuts of U-13. Returning to the Library no longer restarts it, so the roll and search you left are kept. There are no recipe controls yet (U-03 to U-05); `useDevelop.setRecipe(recipe, {dragging})` is the way they will ask for new pictures.

### U-03 – Base and Invert panel with film-base picker

- Milestone: M3 Develop UI | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: feature
- Labels: area:ui, type:feature, size:M
- Depends on: U-02, L-08
- Previous ID: GTP-052 | Spec: specification section 4.2 (Film base, Invert)

**Description**

Panel for the film base (roll default, click or drag-a-box sampling on the rebate) and inversion levels with white-point and black-point pickers, with recipe autosave.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Clicking the rebate sets a manual base for the frame (the median of a small area around the click); dragging a box on the rebate sets it from that region; the roll default can be set from a frame
- [ ] Auto levels can be re-run; manual black/white values override
- [ ] A white-point picker and a black-point picker set the white and black of the Invert block from the density under a click or dragged box (maths in P-14)
- [ ] Changes autosave through recipe_save (debounced 500 ms)

<details><summary>Copy-paste AI prompt</summary>

```text
You are working on the project "Grain to Pixel" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.

PROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)
- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).
- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.
- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.

TECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)
- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.
- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.
- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.
- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.
- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.

ISSUE U-03 – Base and Invert panel with film-base picker
Milestone: M3 Develop UI | Area: User interface | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)
Spec references: specification section 4.2 (Film base, Invert)
Dependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): U-02 – Develop canvas, histogram and before/after; L-08 – Recipe save and apply to frames

DESCRIPTION
Panel for the film base (roll default, click or drag-a-box sampling on the rebate) and inversion levels with white-point and black-point pickers, with recipe autosave.

TECHNICAL DETAILS
FILES: apps/desktop/src/features/develop/panels/BaseInvertPanel.tsx; packages/ui/src/Slider.tsx (Radix).
ROUTES used: develop_analyze, roll_set_film_base, recipe_save.
MIGRATIONS: none.
TESTS: panel behaviour with mocked ipc, autosave debounce, axe.
DELIVERY (decided when U-03 was approved): two parts, as for U-01. Part 1: the film-base picker (click and dragged box), the roll default, black and white sliders per channel, re-run auto levels, and autosave. Part 2, after P-14: the white-point and black-point pickers, which need `levels_from_sample` (P-14 is not in this issue's dependencies, though its criteria use it). Gaps found against the plan and closed in part 1: the preview is cropped, so while picking the canvas shows the whole scan (crop and geometry forced to identity for the preview only); sampling the scan needed a command (`develop_sample_base`, using `sample_film_base`); auto levels needed a base to fit (`develop_analyze` takes an optional base, and `DevelopAnalysis.base_from_roll` became `base_given`); and setting the roll's base regenerates the thumbnails of the frames that follow it (`frames_following_roll_base`). `Slider` uses Radix (`@radix-ui/react-slider`); a key press is reported as a release at once, so the sharp picture is not left behind a rough one. The view also listens to library events now, so the filmstrip's thumbnails follow an edit. Part 2 (decided when it was approved): the white and black pickers measure the cropped frame without turns, flips or straightening (the canvas shows it that way while picking), through `develop_sample_levels` and `levels_from_sample`; picking on the final, turned picture would need the inverse of the geometry stage and is not done. The neutral picker stays with U-04.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
- Clicking the rebate sets a manual base for the frame (the median of a small area around the click); dragging a box on the rebate sets it from that region; the roll default can be set from a frame
- Auto levels can be re-run; manual black/white values override
- A white-point picker and a black-point picker set the white and black of the Invert block from the density under a click or dragged box (maths in P-14)
- Changes autosave through recipe_save (debounced 500 ms)

Needs from the human: approval of your proposal.
Definition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.
```

</details>

TECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)
- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.
- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.
- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.
- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.
- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.

Develop-panel controls for dust removal (enable, threshold, grow) shown only when the frame has an IR plane.

- [ ] Visible dust removal on a real scan confirmed by the human
- [ ] axe clean

<details><summary>Copy-paste AI prompt</summary>

```text
You are working on the project "Grain to Pixel" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.

PROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)
- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).
- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.
- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.

TECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)
- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.
- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.
- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.
- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.
- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.

### H-07 – Scanlight V4 serial driver and FakeLight

- Milestone: M6 Guided RGB capture | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: feature
- Labels: area:hardware, type:feature, size:M
- Depends on: R-02
- Previous ID: GTP-080 | Spec: specification section 6.1; ADR 0004; docs/research/scanlight-capture.md

**Description**

Binary protocol driver for the Scanlight V4 over USB CDC serial, with a recording fake for tests.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Packet encode/decode is byte-exact against the vectors recorded in R-02
- [ ] Decoder never panics on truncated or garbage input; unknown headers dropped; lengths checked
- [ ] SET_COLOR always sends save flag 0; there is no SET_TRIM; levels clamped 0..=255
- [ ] Port probing with GET_FW_VERSION accepts firmware IDs 1 and 2 and warns otherwise

<details><summary>Copy-paste AI prompt</summary>

```text
You are working on the project "Grain to Pixel" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.

PROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)
- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).
- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.
- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.

TECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)
- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.
- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.
- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.
- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.
- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.

ISSUE H-07 – Scanlight V4 serial driver and FakeLight
Milestone: M6 Guided RGB capture | Area: Hardware ingest and capture | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)
Spec references: specification section 6.1; ADR 0004; docs/research/scanlight-capture.md
Dependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): R-02 – Spike - Scanlight V4 serial control

DESCRIPTION
Binary protocol driver for the Scanlight V4 over USB CDC serial, with a recording fake for tests.

TECHNICAL DETAILS
FILES (crates/gtp-capture/src): light.rs (trait LightSource, LightInfo, LightStatus), scanlight/{mod.rs, protocol.rs, port.rs}, fakes.rs (FakeLight).
INTERFACES: `trait LightSource { fn connect(&mut self, port: Option<&str>) -> Result<LightInfo>; fn set_levels(&mut self, rgb: [u8; 3]) -> Result<()>; fn shutter_pulse(&mut self, tens_of_ms: u8) -> Result<()>; fn status(&self) -> LightStatus; }`. Packets `0xFE`, header, length, data: SET_COLOR (0), GET_FW_VERSION (2), SHUTTER_PULSE (3), SET_FOCUS (7); device packets LED_TEMP (1), VBUS (2), FW_VERSION (3). `serialport` crate; background reader updates status.
ROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.
TESTS: byte-exact vectors, garbage input robustness, save flag never 1, FakeLight records commands. check-deps: gtp-capture depends on gtp-core only.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
- Packet encode/decode is byte-exact against the vectors recorded in R-02
- Decoder never panics on truncated or garbage input; unknown headers dropped; lengths checked
- SET_COLOR always sends save flag 0; there is no SET_TRIM; levels clamped 0..=255
- Port probing with GET_FW_VERSION accepts firmware IDs 1 and 2 and warns otherwise

Needs from the human: approval of your proposal.
Definition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.
```

</details>

### H-08 – Capture orchestrator, CameraSource, FakeCamera and safety

- Milestone: M6 Guided RGB capture | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: feature
- Labels: area:hardware, type:feature, size:M
- Depends on: H-07, L-01, H-06
- Previous ID: GTP-081 | Spec: specification section 6.1; ADR 0004

**Description**

The per-frame R, G, B sequence with camera port, fake camera and the hardware-safety rules.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Sequence order is R, G, B with the configured settle delay; light is off after success, cancel, error, timeout and panic
- [ ] Temperature above temp_abort_c aborts the sequence; above temp_warn_c emits a warning
- [ ] EXIF mismatch and clipping warnings are produced; a triplet frame is stored via the merge path
- [ ] Fakes allow the whole job to run in tests

### U-10 – Capture view

- Milestone: M6 Guided RGB capture | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: feature
- Labels: area:ui, type:feature, size:M, needs:hw
- Depends on: D-04, U-09
- Previous ID: GTP-085 | Spec: specification sections 6.1, 7, 11, 13

**Description**

Capture section of the Import view: connection status, per-colour level sliders, test shot with histogram, Next frame button, result and counter.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Status badges for light and camera; sliders per colour; test shot with histogram
- [ ] Large Next frame button (Space) runs capture_triplet and shows the merged result and a roll frame counter
- [ ] Warning banners for temperature, EXIF mismatch and clipping; the light turns off when the view closes
- [ ] Capture per frame (excluding exposure) is at most 15 s on the author's rig (human reports); axe clean

<details><summary>Copy-paste AI prompt</summary>

```text
You are working on the project "Grain to Pixel" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.

PROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)
- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).
- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.
- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.

TECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)
- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.
- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.
- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.
- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.
- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.

- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.
- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.
- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.

### T-07 – Make the Windows WebDriver e2e run on CI runners

- Milestone: M0 Foundations | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: test
- Labels: area:tooling, type:test, size:M
- Depends on: T-04
- Previous ID: none | Spec: specification section 8; convention section 8

**Description**

The WebDriver e2e (tauri-driver + msedgedriver) passes locally but cannot start a session on GitHub-hosted Windows runners, so it is a manual/weekly workflow instead of a required check. Find a way to run it on CI and make it required.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Root cause is documented: on the runner (elevated session, High integrity) WebView2 ignores WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS, so no --remote-debugging-port and no DevToolsActivePort
- [ ] One of these is implemented and proven: an app-side test-only debug-port hook behind a Cargo feature, a throwaway standard user for the test step, or a self-hosted or other runner
- [ ] The e2e job passes on a pull request and is added to the required checks of develop
- [ ] e2e-windows.yml is folded back into ci.yml (or removed) and docs/convention.md section 8 is updated

### P-14 – Picker maths - white, black and neutral points

- Milestone: M3 Develop UI | Estimate: 0.5d (size S) | Type: feature
- Labels: area:pipeline, type:feature, size:S
- Depends on: P-06, P-08
- Previous ID: none | Spec: specification sections 4.1 and 4.2 steps 5 and 6

**Description**

The functions behind the Develop view pickers: set the Invert white or black point from a sampled area, and compute colour axes that make a sampled grey neutral.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] neutral_balance returns red_cyan, green_magenta and blue_yellow values inside the Recipe ranges; applying them to the sample gives equal channels within 1 percent
- [ ] An empty, black or non-finite sample returns InvalidInput; nothing panics

### P-15 – Output sharpening

- Milestone: M3 Develop UI | Estimate: 0.5d (size S) | Type: feature
- Labels: area:pipeline, type:feature, size:S
- Depends on: P-09
- Previous ID: none | Spec: specification section 4.2 step 9

**Description**

An unsharp mask applied to the final resized image at export, with amount and radius in the Recipe output block.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Amount 0 is the identity; a flat image stays flat; it never runs on the working image, only after the final resize
- [ ] The Recipe output block gains sharpen.amount (0 to 150) and sharpen.radius_px (0.3 to 3) with clamping, and the spec lists them
- [ ] The radius is defined relative to the output size and documented

### P-16 – Flat-field correction for camera copies

- Milestone: M5 RGB triplets (files) | Estimate: 1d (size M) | Type: feature
- Labels: area:pipeline, type:feature, size:M
- Depends on: H-05
- Previous ID: none | Spec: specification sections 4.2 step 1 and 11

**Description**

Correct uneven illumination from the light panel in RGB triplet captures, using a reference exposure of the empty light, before the film base is normalized.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] A reference exposure per colour channel is stored with the roll or capture session
- [ ] Dividing by the normalized reference makes a synthetic shaded flat field flat within 1 percent; a uniform reference is the identity
- [ ] A reference with zero, negative or non-finite values is rejected with InvalidInput

### P-17 – Develop preview speed for large proxies

- Milestone: M3 Develop UI | Estimate: 0.5d (size S) | Type: feature
- Labels: area:pipeline, type:feature, size:S
- Depends on: P-11
- Previous ID: none | Spec: specification section 11

**Description**

Bring `develop_render` at 1600 px under the 100 ms budget for every frame shape. A 3:2 frame takes 73 ms but a square one (6x6, a 2048 x 2048 proxy) takes 109 ms, found when P-11 was measured.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Per-stage timings of `develop_render` are recorded for a 3:2 and a square frame (extend the ignored timing test), so the fix targets the real cost
- [ ] A 1600 px render of a square frame takes at most 100 ms and a 3:2 frame is no slower, on the same machine with the same typical edit
- [ ] Output is bit-identical to before (golden harness and the existing render tests pass unchanged)
- [ ] No new dependency, no schema or IPC change

ISSUE P-17 – Develop preview speed for large proxies
Milestone: M3 Develop UI | Area: Image pipeline | Type: feature | Size: S (0.5d)
Spec references: specification section 11
Dependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): P-11 – Develop service - open, analyze and render

DESCRIPTION
Bring `develop_render` at 1600 px under the 100 ms budget for every frame shape. A 3:2 frame takes 73 ms but a square one (6x6, a 2048 x 2048 proxy) takes 109 ms, found when P-11 was measured.

TECHNICAL DETAILS
FILES: crates/gtp-app/src/pipeline/ and crates/gtp-negative/src/ (the stages that turn out to be serial); crates/gtp-app/src/services/develop.rs (timing test).
APPROACH: measure first. Colour, tone and geometry already use rayon; the candidates are the base normalize and invert stages, the downscale of the proxy on every render (`build_proxy`), the crop copy and the RGBA conversion. Parallelise the serial ones and avoid the repeated downscale when a render is at or near proxy size.
TESTS: existing render and golden tests unchanged; timing run recorded in the PR (3:2 and square, per stage).
NOTES: added after P-11 and L-08 were delivered, so it is GitHub issue #130, not 74 (issue and pull request numbers share one sequence). Use 130 as its `number` in docs/progress.md.
DELIVERY (decided when P-17 was approved): measured first, per stage (see the timing test in `pipeline/mod.rs`). The two costs that mattered were the per-render downscale of the proxy and the conversion to 8-bit sRGB; normalize and invert are small. Done: the conversion looks each level up from 255 switch-over points found by bisection on the real formula (checked for every float from 0 to 1 in an ignored test, and around each threshold in the normal ones), and the downscale copies its pixels in and out on all cores. The library's own `rayon` feature was tried and dropped: it pulls in the `image` crate with its codecs (about 50 packages) and made no difference to the total. The fallback of keeping a 1600 px proxy for the open frame was not needed, so memory is unchanged. Both changes are checked bit for bit against the code they replace.

ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
- Per-stage timings of `develop_render` are recorded for a 3:2 and a square frame (extend the ignored timing test), so the fix targets the real cost
- A 1600 px render of a square frame takes at most 100 ms and a 3:2 frame is no slower, on the same machine with the same typical edit
- Output is bit-identical to before (golden harness and the existing render tests pass unchanged)
- No new dependency, no schema or IPC change

Needs from the human: approval of your proposal.
Definition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.
```

</details>
