## JSON for bulk import

A valid JSON array, in upload order. Fields: `id`, `title`, `description`, `acceptance_criteria`, `estimate`, `labels`, `milestone`, `dependencies`, `prompt`, `body`. `body` (description, criteria, estimate, dependencies, references) contains `{{dep:ID}}` placeholders that the upload script replaces with GitHub issue numbers; the script appends `prompt` to the body in a collapsible section so each GitHub issue is self-contained.

Allowed edges: core -> none; io, negative, edit, catalog, capture -> core; ingest -> core, io; app -> all gtp crates; src-tauri -> app, core; tools/benchmark -> app, core.\nROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: integration test: check-deps passes on this workspace, fails on the bad fixture; a deliberate `unwrap()` fails clippy (show, then remove).\nPREREQUISITES (human): pnpm installed.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- rust-toolchain.toml pins stable; `[workspace.lints]` denies unwrap_used, expect_used, panic, todo, print_stdout, print_stderr, dbg_macro and forbids unsafe_code; every crate inherits them\n- `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo deny check` pass\n- ESLint (typescript-eslint, jsx-a11y, no-console, no literal strings in JSX), Prettier and a strict tsconfig base exist; `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test` run\n- `tools/scripts/check-deps` enforces the allowed-edges table of architecture.md section 3.3, passes on the workspace and fails on a bad fixture\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",
    "body": "## Description\nMake the convention rules enforceable locally: pinned toolchain, workspace lints, formatters, licence policy, ESLint/Prettier/tsconfig, and a script that enforces the allowed crate dependency edges.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] rust-toolchain.toml pins stable; `[workspace.lints]` denies unwrap_used, expect_used, panic, todo, print_stdout, print_stderr, dbg_macro and forbids unsafe_code; every crate inherits them\n- [ ] `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo deny check` pass\n- [ ] ESLint (typescript-eslint, jsx-a11y, no-console, no literal strings in JSX), Prettier and a strict tsconfig base exist; `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test` run\n- [ ] `tools/scripts/check-deps` enforces the allowed-edges table of architecture.md section 3.3, passes on the workspace and fails on a bad fixture\n\n## Estimate\n1d (size M)\n\n## Dependencies\n- {{dep:T-01}} T-01 – Initialize Git repository and LFS\n\n## References\n- Spec: convention sections 1, 3, 8; architecture section 3.3\n- Milestone: M0 Foundations; area: Tooling, CI and release; previous ID: GTP-002"
  },

    "title": "D-01 – Tauri 2 desktop skeleton",
    "description": "Scaffold apps/desktop (React + TypeScript + Vite UI, Tauri 2 shell) so a window launches on Windows and macOS with the project layout, strict CSP and minimal capabilities.",

    "prompt": "You are working on the project \"Grain to Pixel\" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.\n\nPROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)\n- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).\n- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.\n- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.\n\nTECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)\n- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.\n- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE D-01 – Tauri 2 desktop skeleton\nMilestone: M0 Foundations | Area: Desktop shell (Tauri) | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)\nSpec references: specification sections 2, 3, 9; architecture sections 3.1, 4\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): T-02 – Lint, format and dependency-rule tooling\n\nDESCRIPTION\nScaffold apps/desktop (React + TypeScript + Vite UI, Tauri 2 shell) so a window launches on Windows and macOS with the project layout, strict CSP and minimal capabilities.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES: apps/desktop/{package.json, vite.config.ts, tsconfig.json, index.html}; src/{main.tsx, App.tsx}; src/app/store.ts (Zustand, view = 'library' | 'develop' | 'import'); src/features/{library,develop,import}/*View.tsx placeholders using t(); src/shared/{ipc.ts, i18n/index.ts, i18n/en.json, styles/tokens.css}; src-tauri/{Cargo.toml, tauri.conf.json, build.rs, capabilities/default.json}; src-tauri/src/{main.rs, lib.rs, state.rs, commands/mod.rs, commands/system.rs}.\nROUTES: stub `library_info() -> LibraryInfo { app_version }`.\nMIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: Vitest renders App and switches views; manual launch recorded.\nPREREQUISITES (human): Windows needs WebView2 and MSVC build tools; macOS needs Xcode command line tools.\nOUT OF SCOPE: real features, CI.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- `pnpm dev` opens the window on Windows; the macOS launch is confirmed by the human\n- CSP is `default-src 'self'`, no shell plugin, capabilities list only what the stub needs\n- src-tauri is a Cargo workspace member and passes the workspace lints and check-deps\n- A UI test renders the app shell and switches between Library, Develop and Import views\n\nNeeds from the human: a macOS check. Prepare exact steps and a checklist; never tick these items yourself.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",

    "prompt": "You are working on the project \"Grain to Pixel\" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.\n\nPROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)\n- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).\n- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.\n- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.\n\nTECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)\n- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.\n- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE D-03 – Logging, panic hook and client error reporting\nMilestone: M0 Foundations | Area: Desktop shell (Tauri) | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)\nSpec references: specification section 12; convention sections 5 and 6\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): D-02 – Typed IPC and AppError\n\nDESCRIPTION\nObservability baseline: rolling JSON logs, a panic hook with crash notes, a client-event command and React error handling.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES: apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/logging.rs (`init(log_dir, dev)`, `job_span(job_id, kind)`, `stage_span(stage)`); src-tauri/src/lib.rs panic hook writing crash-<timestamp>.txt and a last_crash marker, emitting `app://crash-report` next start; commands/system.rs `log_client_event(level: LogLevel, msg: String)`; apps/desktop/src/app/error-boundary.tsx and window.onerror / unhandledrejection hooks.\nTYPES: `enum LogLevel { Error, Warn, Info, Debug }`.\nMIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: rotation with a tiny cap in a temp dir; subprocess panic test; Vitest for the error boundary; log_client_event writes level and message.\nOUT OF SCOPE: diagnostics zip (D-05).\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- A log file is created in the OS log dir and rotated daily, keeping 7 days with a 50 MB cap (tested with a small cap)\n- A panic in a subprocess test leaves a crash note and the next start reports it\n- A client error reaches the log with level and message; UI text never shows a stack trace\n- No println!/console.log remain (lints pass)\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",

Device packets: LED_TEMP (1, int32 millidegrees C), VBUS (2, mV), FW_VERSION (3). No acks, no VID/PID: probe ports with GET_FW_VERSION. Throwaway script (python/pyserial or Rust) outside crates/.\nOUTPUT: docs/research/scanlight-capture.md, ADR 0004 amendments, recorded packets as test vectors, follow-up issues. Prepare exact steps and a checklist; never tick these items yourself.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",

    "body": "## Description\nDecide whether the libgphoto2 camera backend is viable on Apple Silicon: D600 support, capture and download of a NEF, USB claim problems, a Rust binding, and how to bundle the libraries in the .app.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] D600 supported; a NEF captured and downloaded by command line (human reports)\n- [ ] macOS USB-claim workaround documented\n- [ ] A Rust binding compiles and lists the camera\n- [ ] Bundling approach written down (dylibs, camera/port driver dirs, env paths, ad-hoc signing) with a go/no-go for the M6 cut line\n- [ ] docs/research/scanlight-capture.md updated and ADR 0004 amended\n\n## Estimate\n1d (size M)\n\n## Dependencies\n- {{dep:T-01}} T-01 – Initialize Git repository and LFS\n\n## References\n- Spec: specification section 6.1; ADR 0004\n- Milestone: M0 Foundations; area: Research spikes; previous ID: GTP-010"
  },

    "prompt": "You are working on the project \"Grain to Pixel\" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.\n\nPROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)\n- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).\n- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.\n- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.\n\nTECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)\n- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.\n- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE R-04 – Spike - digiCamControl on Windows\nMilestone: M0 Foundations | Area: Research spikes | Type: spike | Size: S (0.5d)\nSpec references: specification section 6.1\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): R-02 – Spike - Scanlight V4 serial control\n\nDESCRIPTION\nConfirm the Windows capture path: Scanlight pulse fires the D600, digiCamControl downloads the NEF into a session folder, and the app can watch that folder.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nSCOPE: configure digiCamControl session folder; fire with SHUTTER_PULSE (length from R-02); observe temp/partial file naming and stable-size timing; note D600 support status of digiCamControl.\nOUTPUT: research note; recommendation for the WatchedFolder file-complete rule.\nPROCESS: human runs; assistant records.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- D600 recognized by digiCamControl; pulse-fired shots appear in the chosen folder without digiCamControl triggering them\n- Typical file write time and partial-file behaviour recorded\n- docs/research/scanlight-capture.md updated\n\nNeeds from the human: real hardware run and report. Prepare exact steps and a checklist; never tick these items yourself.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",

validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE P-03 – TIFF read/write with input limits\nMilestone: M1 Pipeline on files | Area: Image pipeline | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)\nSpec references: specification sections 5.1 and 9; convention section 4\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): P-01 – Core domain types and Recipe v1\n\nDESCRIPTION\nSafe 16-bit TIFF reading and writing in gtp-io for scans, IR planes and library originals, with size limits checked before allocation.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES (crates/gtp-io): src/{lib.rs, limits.rs, tiff.rs, hash.rs}.\nINTERFACES: `Limits { max_side: 20_000, max_decoded_bytes: 2 GiB }`; `read_tiff16(path, &Limits) -> Result<Tiff16>`; `Tiff16 { width, height, channels, data: Vec<u16> }`; `to_f32_linear(&Tiff16) -> ImageF32` (scale only, no gamma); `write_tiff16(path, &Tiff16, Compression::DeflatePredictor)`; `mark_read_only(path)`; `sha256_file(path) -> Result<String>`.\nROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: round trip, crafted oversize header, corrupt/truncated files, compression ratio.\nOUT OF SCOPE: NEF decode (H-05), ICC (P-04).\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- Round trip is bit-exact for RGB and gray\n- A crafted header with huge dimensions is rejected without a large allocation\n- Corrupt and truncated files return typed errors and never panic\n- Deflate + predictor output is smaller than raw on the synthetic image\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",
    "body": "## Description\nSafe 16-bit TIFF reading and writing in gtp-io for scans, IR planes and library originals, with size limits checked before allocation.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] Round trip is bit-exact for RGB and gray\n- [ ] A crafted header with huge dimensions is rejected without a large allocation\n- [ ] Corrupt and truncated files return typed errors and never panic\n- [ ] Deflate + predictor output is smaller than raw on the synthetic image\n\n## Estimate\n1d (size M)\n\n## Dependencies\n- {{dep:P-01}} P-01 – Core domain types and Recipe v1\n\n## References\n- Spec: specification sections 5.1 and 9; convention section 4\n- Milestone: M1 Pipeline on files; area: Image pipeline; previous ID: GTP-022"

    "prompt": "You are working on the project \"Grain to Pixel\" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.\n\nPROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)\n- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).\n- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.\n- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.\n\nTECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)\n- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.\n- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE P-04 – Output colour transform and export encoders\nMilestone: M1 Pipeline on files | Area: Image pipeline | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)\nSpec references: specification sections 4.2 step 9 and 5\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): P-03 – TIFF read/write with input limits\n\nDESCRIPTION\nConvert the linear Rec.2020 working space to sRGB, Adobe RGB or ProPhoto with ICC embedded, and encode 16-bit TIFF and JPEG.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES (crates/gtp-io): src/icc.rs, src/export/{tiff16.rs, jpeg.rs}.\nINTERFACES: `enum OutputSpace { Srgb, AdobeRgb, ProPhoto }`; `to_output(&ImageF32, OutputSpace) -> Result<Tiff16Output>` (lcms2); `encode_tiff16(img, space, path)`; `encode_jpeg(img, space, quality, path)`.\nROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: known patch conversions, ICC parse-back, JPEG size monotonic.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- Reference colour patches convert within ΔE2000 of 1 of known values\n- Output files contain the expected ICC profile (parsed back in a test)\n- JPEG quality changes file size monotonically\n- Builds on Windows; macOS confirmed by the human until CI covers it\n\nNeeds from the human: a macOS check. Prepare exact steps and a checklist; never tick these items yourself.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",

Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.\n\nTECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)\n- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.\n- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE P-08 – Colour and tone stages\nMilestone: M1 Pipeline on files | Area: Image pipeline | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)\nSpec references: specification sections 4.1 and 4.2 steps 6-7\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): P-06 – Inversion and auto levels\n\nDESCRIPTION\nTemperature/tint, saturation, exposure, contrast and curves as in-place stages in gtp-edit.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES (crates/gtp-edit/src): lib.rs, color.rs, tone.rs, curve.rs.\nINTERFACES: `apply_color(&mut ImageF32, &Color)` (temperature/tint as per-channel gains, mapping documented; saturation luma-preserving in linear Rec.2020); `apply_tone(&mut ImageF32, &Tone)` (exposure EV, contrast about mid-grey, master + R/G/B curves); `Curve::new(&[(f32, f32)]) -> Result<Curve>` monotone spline with a 4096-entry LUT.\nROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: identity, EV doubling, curve monotonicity, in-place check; criterion bench for apply_tone on 21 MP.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- The default recipe is the identity within 1e-6\n- +1 EV doubles linear values; contrast 0 is identity\n- Curves are monotone for monotone control points; invalid points are rejected or clamped\n- A 21 MP buffer is processed in place with no extra full-size allocation\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",
    "body": "## Description\nTemperature/tint, saturation, exposure, contrast and curves as in-place stages in gtp-edit.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] The default recipe is the identity within 1e-6\n- [ ] +1 EV doubles linear values; contrast 0 is identity\n- [ ] Curves are monotone for monotone control points; invalid points are rejected or clamped\n- [ ] A 21 MP buffer is processed in place with no extra full-size allocation\n\n## Estimate\n1d (size M)\n\n## Dependencies\n- {{dep:P-06}} P-06 – Inversion and auto levels\n\n## References\n- Spec: specification sections 4.1 and 4.2 steps 6-7\n- Milestone: M1 Pipeline on files; area: Image pipeline; previous ID: GTP-027"
  },

Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE Q-02 – Benchmarks against reference renders\nMilestone: M1 Pipeline on files | Area: Quality, docs and release verification | Type: test | Size: M (1d)\nSpec references: specification sections 1, 8, 11\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): Q-01 – Golden-image harness and ΔE2000\n\nDESCRIPTION\nMeasure quality against Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab renders and speed against the budgets.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES: tools/benchmark/src/{reference.rs, report.rs}, tools/benchmark/benches/pipeline.rs; manifest `samples/*/reference/<set>/manifest.toml` with `[[frame]] id, source, kind, render`; docs/research/benchmark.md.\nCLI: `benchmark reference --set <dir>`, `benchmark report`.\nROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: run on synthetic data in CI; on the real references manually.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- At least 10 reference pairs supplied by the human and listed in the manifest\n- The report command prints per-frame and summary ΔE2000 (median, p95)\n- Criterion benches (decode, 1600 px render, analyze, full-resolution render) run and results land in docs/research/benchmark.md\n\nNeeds from the human: files only the human has. Prepare exact steps and a checklist; never tick these items yourself.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",

    "prompt": "You are working on the project \"Grain to Pixel\" in the repository at the current working directory. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE Q-03 – Calibrate quality metric and budgets\nMilestone: M1 Pipeline on files | Area: Quality, docs and release verification | Type: docs | Size: S (0.5d)\nSpec references: specification sections 1, 11, 16\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): Q-02 – Benchmarks against reference renders\n\nDESCRIPTION\nTurn the proposed quality thresholds and performance budgets into measured values and close milestone M1.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nSCOPE: run the benchmark; propose final ΔE2000 thresholds (currently median 6 for auto and 3 after tweaks) and confirm the section 11 budgets on the reference machine. Do not tune the pipeline here; propose follow-up issues for misses.\nFILES: docs/specification.md sections 1 and 11, docs/research/benchmark.md.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- Numbers computed on the human's reference set\n- Spec sections 1 and 11 updated with calibrated values approved by the human\n- The human confirms the M1 exit criteria are met\n\nNeeds from the human: files only the human has.     "body": "## Description\nThe managed library folder (originals, thumbs, exports, library.db), safe path handling, app settings with feature flags, and the library and settings commands.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] `library_create` and `library_open` create and reopen the layout; a newer-version library opens read-only\n- [ ] Paths are canonicalized and must stay inside the library or a user-selected directory\n- [ ] `settings_get/settings_set` round-trip settings.json (last library, window size, flags with defaults from spec section 10)\n- [ ] Free space can be queried\n\n## Estimate\n1d (size M)\n\n## Dependencies\n- {{dep:L-02}} L-02 – Catalog schema, migrations, backup and integrity\n\n## References\n- Spec: specification sections 5.1, 5.3, 5.4, 6, 10\n- Milestone: M2 Library; area: Library and data; previous ID: GTP-042"

    "prompt": "You are working on the project \"Grain to Pixel\" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.\n\nPROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)\n- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).\n- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.\n- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.\n\nTECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)\n- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.\n- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE L-04 – Roll and frame commands\nMilestone: M2 Library | Area: Library and data | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)\nSpec references: specification sections 4, 5.3, 6\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): L-03 – Library create/open, folder layout and settings\n\nDESCRIPTION\nCRUD for rolls and frames over the catalog through gtp-app services and thin Tauri commands.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES: crates/gtp-app/src/services/roll_frame.rs; apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/{roll.rs, frame.rs}.\nROUTES: `roll_create(RollInput) -> Roll`, `roll_update(RollId, RollPatch) -> Roll`, `roll_list() -> Roll[]`, `roll_delete(RollId)`, `roll_set_film_base(RollId, [f32;3]) -> Roll`, `frame_list(FrameQuery { roll_id?, page, page_size }) -> Page<FrameSummary>`, `frame_get(FrameId) -> Frame`, `frame_delete(FrameId[])`, `frame_set_note(FrameId, String)`.\nMIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: services against a temp library, injected transaction failure keeps files, command-layer error mapping.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- All roll and frame commands work with typed errors and regenerated ipc-types\n- Deleting frames or rolls removes files only after the DB transaction commits and leaves files if the transaction fails\n- Names are validated (trimmed, at most 200 chars) and ids parsed\n- Roll create and update set camera, box ISO, shot ISO and the shooting date range (partial dates), validated by `gtp_core::model::{check_iso, check_shot_range}` (fields added in L-02)\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",

Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.\n\nPROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)\n- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).\n- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.\n- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.\n\nTECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)\n- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.\n- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE L-07 – Library verify job\nMilestone: M2 Library | Area: Library and data | Type: feature | Size: S (0.5d)\nSpec references: specification section 5.3\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): L-05 – TIFF import job with hashing and thumbnails\n\nDESCRIPTION\nRe-hash all originals and list missing or corrupt files.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES: crates/gtp-app/src/services/verify.rs (`verify_library`, report-only, decided when L-07 was approved); crates/gtp-catalog/src/repo/verify.rs; gtp-io `sha256_file_with` (progress and cancel inside a file); src-tauri/src/commands/library.rs.\nROUTES: `library_verify() -> JobId`; the job result is `Verified(VerifyResult { files_checked, bytes_checked, problems[{frame_id, roll_id, role, rel_path, reason: Missing|Changed|WrongSize|Unreadable, details}], thumbnails_missing, stray_folders[] })`. `LibraryDetails` gains `last_verify_at` and `last_verify_problems`.\nMIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: tamper/delete detection, cancel.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- Detects a tampered and a deleted original\n- Runs as a cancellable job with progress\n- Result lists affected frames with reasons\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",

validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE P-11 – Develop service - open, analyze and render\nMilestone: M3 Develop UI | Area: Image pipeline | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)\nSpec references: specification sections 3, 6, 11; architecture section 3.5\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): P-10 – Application layer gtp-app - pipeline, proxy and analyze; L-04 – Roll and frame commands; L-05 – TIFF import job with hashing and thumbnails\n\nDESCRIPTION\nBackend for interactive editing: load a frame, build and cache the proxy, analyze, and render previews over binary IPC with stale-request dropping.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES: crates/gtp-app/src/services/develop.rs; apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/develop.rs.\nROUTES: `develop_open(FrameId) -> DevelopInfo { width, height, has_ir, recipe, roll_base }`, `develop_analyze(FrameId) -> Analysis`, `develop_render(FrameId, Recipe, max_edge: u32, seq: u64) -> binary` (16-byte header: seq u64, width u32, height u32; then RGBA8) via `tauri::ipc::Response`.\nVALIDATION: max_edge in [256, 4096].\nMIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: open/render on synthetic frames, header layout, LRU eviction at the 5th frame, superseded render, clamping, timing.\nDELIVERY (decided when P-11 was approved): a superseded render is dropped by checking its sequence number before it starts (after waiting for the frame's render turn) and again before it returns, so no change to the pipeline stages; cooperative cancellation inside the stages is left for later if the budget needs it. The analysis type is named `DevelopAnalysis` on the IPC boundary. The infrared plane is not loaded yet (`has_ir` is reported; dust removal, P-13, will load it). `develop_render` is a raw-bytes command, routed through `ipc::RAW_COMMANDS` like `frame_thumbnail`; `shared/ipc.ts` gets `developOpen`, `developAnalyze` and `developRender` (with the header parsed), without any UI.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- `develop_open` loads the original and caches a 2048 px proxy in an LRU of 4\n- `develop_render` returns RGBA8 sRGB bytes with a header and drops superseded requests as Cancelled\n- 1600 px render time recorded against the 100 ms budget; recipe clamped server-side\n- No pixel data in logs; ipc-types regenerated\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",

Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.\n\nTECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)\n- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.\n- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE L-09 – Regenerate thumbnail when a recipe changes\nMilestone: M3 Develop UI | Area: Library and data | Type: feature | Size: S (0.5d)\nSpec references: specification section 5.1 (thumbnails are a rebuildable cache)\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): L-08 – Recipe save and apply to frames; L-05 – TIFF import job with hashing and thumbnails\n\nDESCRIPTION\nAudit gap. Thumbnails must reflect the saved recipe; regenerate the 512 px thumbnail in the background after recipe_save and for applied frames.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES: crates/gtp-app/src/services/develop.rs (thumbnail task using `pipeline::render` Preview at 512 px and `gtp-io` JPEG encode).\nROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: thumbnail changes after recipe change (pixel difference), debounce, rebuild-if-missing.\nDELIVERY (decided when L-09 was approved): the grid learns which pictures changed from an optional list of frame ids on the `library://changed` payload (`frame_ids`, empty for other changes), so only those cached images are dropped and loaded again, with the change event sent when a batch is done (the shared `LibraryEvents` trait gets `frames_changed`). A missing thumbnail is rebuilt inside `frame_thumbnail` while the grid waits, one rebuild at a time. The service is `services/thumbnails.rs` (not `develop.rs`), with one worker thread and a 500 ms quiet period; a thumbnail is rendered from the cached proxy when the frame is open, otherwise from the original decoded without caching it, so a batch cannot evict the frame being edited. A failure keeps the old thumbnail and is logged (gtp-app gains the `tracing` dependency); a read-only library is never written to.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- After recipe_save the thumbnail file is replaced and `library://changed` is emitted\n- Regeneration is debounced and never blocks recipe_save\n- A missing thumbnail is rebuilt on demand\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",
    "body": "## Description\nAudit gap. Thumbnails must reflect the saved recipe; regenerate the 512 px thumbnail in the background after recipe_save and for applied frames.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] After recipe_save the thumbnail file is replaced and `library://changed` is emitted\n- [ ] Regeneration is debounced and never blocks recipe_save\n- [ ] A missing thumbnail is rebuilt on demand\n\n## Estimate\n0.5d (size S)\n\n## Dependencies\n- {{dep:L-08}} L-08 – Recipe save and apply to frames\n- {{dep:L-05}} L-05 – TIFF import job with hashing and thumbnails\n\n## References\n- Spec: specification section 5.1 (thumbnails are a rebuildable cache)\n- Milestone: M3 Develop UI; area: Library and data; previous ID: new"
  },

Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE U-02 – Develop canvas, histogram and before/after\nMilestone: M3 Develop UI | Area: User interface | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)\nSpec references: specification sections 3, 7, 13\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): P-11 – Develop service - open, analyze and render\n\nDESCRIPTION\nThe Develop view shell: canvas fed by binary IPC with pan/zoom, filmstrip, histogram and a before/after toggle.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES: apps/desktop/src/features/develop/{DevelopView.tsx, Canvas.tsx, Filmstrip.tsx, use-develop.ts, develop.module.css}; packages/ui/src/Histogram.tsx.\nROUTES used: develop_open, develop_analyze, develop_render.\nMIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: use-develop (debounce, seq drop, drag vs release sizes with mocked ipc), canvas and histogram component tests, axe.\nDELIVERY (decided when U-02 was approved): \"before\" is the automatic conversion (the recipe from `develop_analyze`), which stays the same however often the frame is saved. Zoom runs from fit to 400% of the 2048 px proxy (past 100% pixels are enlarged, not smoothed); true 1:1 inspection at full resolution would need a full-resolution tile render, which is not in the plan. A frame opens with a double-click or Enter in the Library grid, and the filmstrip lists the frames of the Library's current view (the same query as the grid, shared through `use-library-query`). The histogram is computed in the browser from the preview's pixels, so no backend change was needed. `Histogram` is drawn as SVG in `packages/ui`. Keys beyond the two in the criteria: Escape (back to the Library) and 0, 1, + and - (zoom), all in one map (`develop-keys.ts`) for the customizable shortcuts of U-13. Returning to the Library no longer restarts it, so the roll and search you left are kept. There are no recipe controls yet (U-03 to U-05); `useDevelop.setRecipe(recipe, {dragging})` is the way they will ask for new pictures.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- The canvas draws develop_render output; drag requests 1600 px and release requests 2048 px\n- Stale results are dropped by sequence number\n- `\\` toggles before/after; `[` `]` move through the filmstrip\n- vitest-axe clean; all text via t()\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Part 1: the film-base picker (click and dragged box), the roll default, black and white sliders per channel, re-run auto levels, and autosave. Part 2, after P-14: the white-point and black-point pickers, which need `levels_from_sample` (P-14 is not in this issue's dependencies, though its criteria use it). Gaps found against the plan and closed in part 1: the preview is cropped, so while picking the canvas shows the whole scan (crop and geometry forced to identity for the preview only); sampling the scan needed a command (`develop_sample_base`, using `sample_film_base`); auto levels needed a base to fit (`develop_analyze` takes an optional base, and `DevelopAnalysis.base_from_roll` became `base_given`); and setting the roll's base regenerates the thumbnails of the frames that follow it (`frames_following_roll_base`). `Slider` uses Radix (`@radix-ui/react-slider`); a key press is reported as a release at once, so the sharp picture is not left behind a rough one. The view also listens to library events now, so the filmstrip's thumbnails follow an edit. Part 2 (decided when it was approved): the white and black pickers measure the cropped frame without turns, flips or straightening (the canvas shows it that way while picking), through `develop_sample_levels` and `levels_from_sample`; picking on the final, turned picture would need the inverse of the geometry stage and is not done. The neutral picker stays with U-04.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- Clicking the rebate sets a manual base for the frame (the median of a small area around the click); dragging a box on the rebate sets it from that region; the roll default can be set from a frame\n- Auto levels can be re-run; manual black/white values override\n- A white-point picker and a black-point picker set the white and black of the Invert block from the density under a click or dragged box (maths in P-14)\n- Changes autosave through recipe_save (debounced 500 ms)\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11.     "prompt": "You are working on the project \"Grain to Pixel\" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.\n\nPROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)\n- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).\n- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.\n- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.\n\nTECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)\n- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.\n- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE U-05 – Crop and geometry UI\nMilestone: M3 Develop UI | Area: User interface | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)\nSpec references: specification section 4.2 (Frame detection)\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): U-02 – Develop canvas, histogram and before/after; L-08 – Recipe save and apply to frames\n\nDESCRIPTION\nCrop overlay with the auto-detected rectangle and a low-confidence hint, plus rotate, flip and straighten.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES: apps/desktop/src/features/develop/panels/GeometryPanel.tsx and a crop overlay inside Canvas.tsx.\nROUTES used: develop_analyze, develop_render, recipe_save.\nMIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: overlay math, hint on low confidence (mocked analysis), keyboard nudging.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- The overlay shows the auto-detected crop; low confidence shows a hint and the full frame\n- Rotate 90, flips and straighten angle work and autosave\n- Keyboard nudging of the crop works; axe clean\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",
    "body": "## Description\nCrop overlay with the auto-detected rectangle and a low-confidence hint, plus rotate, flip and straighten.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] The overlay shows the auto-detected crop; low confidence shows a hint and the full frame\n- [ ] Rotate 90, flips and straighten angle work and autosave\n- [ ] Keyboard nudging of the crop works; axe clean\n\n## Estimate\n1d (size M)\n\n## Dependencies\n- {{dep:U-02}} U-02 – Develop canvas, histogram and before/after\n- {{dep:L-08}} L-08 – Recipe save and apply to frames\n\n## References\n- Spec: specification section 4.2 (Frame detection)\n- Milestone: M3 Develop UI; area: User interface; previous ID: GTP-054"

Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.\n\nTECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)\n- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.\n- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE H-01 – ScannerSource port and FixtureSource\nMilestone: M4 LS-50 ingest | Area: Hardware ingest and capture | Type: feature | Size: S (0.5d)\nSpec references: specification section 6 (Scanner abstraction)\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): L-05 – TIFF import job with hashing and thumbnails\n\nDESCRIPTION\nThe scanner port with validated parameters and a fixture adapter that replays TIFFs so scan jobs are testable without hardware.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES: crates/gtp-ingest/src/{scanner.rs, fixture.rs}.\nINTERFACES: `trait ScannerSource { fn list_devices(&self) -> Result<Vec<DeviceInfo>>; fn scan(&self, p: &ScanParams, on_frame: &mut dyn FnMut(u32, RawScan) -> Result<()>, progress: &dyn Fn(f32), cancel: &CancelToken) -> Result<()>; }`; `ScanParams { device_id, frames: RangeInclusive<u32>, dpi: u32 (default 4000), samples: u8 (1|2|4|8|16), ir: bool, autofocus: bool }`; `RawScan { rgb16, ir16: Option<_>, dpi, meta }`.\nROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: parameter validation, fixture replay order, cancel.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- `ScanParams` validation rejects frames outside 1..=40, unsupported dpi and invalid sample counts\n- FixtureSource replays TIFFs from samples/ through the same trait\n- `GTP_FAKE_HW=1` selects fixture adapters at app start\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",

    "prompt": "You are working on the project \"Grain to Pixel\" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.\n\nPROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)\n- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).\n- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.\n- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.\n\nTECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)\n- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.\n- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE U-08 – Scanner import UI\nMilestone: M4 LS-50 ingest | Area: User interface | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)\nSpec references: specification section 7\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): H-03 – Roll-scan job - per-frame commit, cancel and resume; U-01 – Library view\n\nDESCRIPTION\nScanner section of the Import view: device, frame range, dpi, samples, IR, per-frame progress, cancel and resume.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES: apps/desktop/src/features/import/{ImportView.tsx, ScannerSection.tsx}.\nROUTES used: scanner_list, scan_start, job_cancel.\nTESTS: mocked ipc for progress, cancel, resume, validation messages, axe.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- Shows the device list and scan settings with validation\n- Per-frame progress list updates from job events; cancel and resume from next frame work\n- axe clean; strings via t()\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE U-09 – Triplet files import UI\nMilestone: M5 RGB triplets (files) | Area: User interface | Type: feature | Size: S (0.5d)\nSpec references: specification section 7\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): H-06 – Triplet import job; U-01 – Library view\n\nDESCRIPTION\nFiles section of the Import view with grouping preview, reorder and confirm.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES: apps/desktop/src/features/import/FilesSection.tsx.\nROUTES used: triplet_group, import_files, job_cancel.\nTESTS: grouping preview, reorder, confirm with mocked ipc, axe.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- Shows candidate triplets, flags missing/extra shots, lets the user reorder and confirm\n- Starts the import job and shows progress\n- axe clean; strings via t()\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11.     "prompt": "You are working on the project \"Grain to Pixel\" in the repository at the current working directory. Follow docs/workflow.md: read this issue, post a short proposal (understanding, approach, files, tests, docs, risks, open questions) and STOP for my explicit approval before writing code. After approval implement with tests and docs, run the CI gates from docs/workflow.md section 8, open a PR that closes the GitHub issue, then report and ask for the next step.\n\nPROJECT CONTEXT (from docs/product/idea.md)\n- Grain to Pixel is a personal Windows 10/11 and macOS desktop app for close friends only, no monetization: an all-in-one tool to scan, convert, edit and organize film negatives (commercial licences are prohibitive).\n- Core workflow: ingest via Nikon Coolscan LS-50 (RGB + IR; the author's modded scanner scans whole rolls of up to 40 frames) or via separate R/G/B exposures shot with a Nikon D600 (NEF) under a Scanlight V4 light, adding tags -> determine frame area and measure film base -> convert to positive -> edit colour balance, tone, geometry, IR/ICE dust removal -> searchable tagged archive.\n- Non-goals: masking, AI generation, advanced retouching. Success: activation, and results comparable to Nikon Scan 4 / Trichroma Lab. Risk: little colour-negative sample data. Constraint: built with Claude Pro, so keep the session narrow and read only what this issue needs.\n\nTECHNICAL CONTEXT (docs/specification.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/structure.md, docs/convention.md)\n- Stack: Tauri 2; Rust workspace in crates/ (gtp-core, gtp-io, gtp-negative, gtp-edit, gtp-catalog, gtp-ingest, gtp-capture, gtp-app); React 18 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite UI in apps/desktop with Zustand, Radix UI and CSS modules; generated packages/ipc-types (tauri-specta); SQLite catalog (rusqlite bundled, FTS5, forward-only migrations); tracing logs; i18n via t() with en.json only.\n- Architecture: feature crates depend on gtp-core (gtp-ingest also on gtp-io) and never on each other; gtp-app is the application layer that combines them (services, JobManager, pipeline); src-tauri is a thin adapter (validate, call a gtp-app service, map errors to AppError); the UI never touches pixels and receives rendered bytes. Long work runs as cancellable jobs with progress events. Hardware sits behind traits (ScannerSource, LightSource, CameraSource) with fakes.\n- Pipeline (spec 4.2) fixed order: load -> IR dust -> crop -> film-base normalize -> invert -> colour -> tone -> geometry -> output transform; internal f32 linear with Rec.2020 primaries; originals immutable, all edits in the versioned Recipe.\n- Repo layout: apps/desktop (src React UI, src-tauri shell), crates/*, packages/{ipc-types,ui}, tools/{benchmark,scripts}, samples/{public,private}, docs/, decisions/ (ADRs), rules/, prompts/.\n- Conventions: no unwrap/expect/unsafe/println in committed Rust, no console.log or any in TS; validate at boundaries; AppError codes; tracing spans; tests are mandatory (unit, golden, fakes, component); Conventional Commits; performance budgets in spec section 11; security in spec section 9.\n\nISSUE H-07 – Scanlight V4 serial driver and FakeLight\nMilestone: M6 Guided RGB capture | Area: Hardware ingest and capture | Type: feature | Size: M (1d)\nSpec references: specification section 6.1; ADR 0004; docs/research/scanlight-capture.md\nDependencies (must be Done first; if any is not, stop and tell me): R-02 – Spike - Scanlight V4 serial control\n\nDESCRIPTION\nBinary protocol driver for the Scanlight V4 over USB CDC serial, with a recording fake for tests.\n\nTECHNICAL DETAILS\nFILES (crates/gtp-capture/src): light.rs (trait LightSource, LightInfo, LightStatus), scanlight/{mod.rs, protocol.rs, port.rs}, fakes.rs (FakeLight).\nINTERFACES: `trait LightSource { fn connect(&mut self, port: Option<&str>) -> Result<LightInfo>; fn set_levels(&mut self, rgb: [u8; 3]) -> Result<()>; fn shutter_pulse(&mut self, tens_of_ms: u8) -> Result<()>; fn status(&self) -> LightStatus; }`. Packets `0xFE`, header, length, data: SET_COLOR (0), GET_FW_VERSION (2), SHUTTER_PULSE (3), SET_FOCUS (7); device packets LED_TEMP (1), VBUS (2), FW_VERSION (3). `serialport` crate; background reader updates status.\nROUTES / MIGRATIONS: none.\nTESTS: byte-exact vectors, garbage input robustness, save flag never 1, FakeLight records commands. check-deps: gtp-capture depends on gtp-core only.\n\nACCEPTANCE CRITERIA\n- Packet encode/decode is byte-exact against the vectors recorded in R-02\n- Decoder never panics on truncated or garbage input; unknown headers dropped; lengths checked\n- SET_COLOR always sends save flag 0; there is no SET_TRIM; levels clamped 0..=255\n- Port probing with GET_FW_VERSION accepts firmware IDs 1 and 2 and warns otherwise\n\nNeeds from the human: approval of your proposal.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",
    "body": "## Description\nBinary protocol driver for the Scanlight V4 over USB CDC serial, with a recording fake for tests.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] Packet encode/decode is byte-exact against the vectors recorded in R-02\n- [ ] Decoder never panics on truncated or garbage input; unknown headers dropped; lengths checked\n- [ ] SET_COLOR always sends save flag 0; there is no SET_TRIM; levels clamped 0..=255\n- [ ] Port probing with GET_FW_VERSION accepts firmware IDs 1 and 2 and warns otherwise\n\n## Estimate\n1d (size M)\n\n## Dependencies\n- {{dep:R-02}} R-02 – Spike - Scanlight V4 serial control\n\n## References\n- Spec: specification section 6.1; ADR 0004; docs/research/scanlight-capture.md\n- Milestone: M6 Guided RGB capture; area: Hardware ingest and capture; previous ID: GTP-080"
  },

Prepare exact steps and a checklist; never tick these items yourself.\nDefinition of Done: docs/workflow.md section 11. Update the docs this change makes stale. Do not widen scope; propose follow-up issues instead.",
    "body": "## Description\nCamera backend that fires the shutter through the Scanlight pulse and waits for NEFs written to a user-selected folder (digiCamControl on Windows).\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] trigger sends SHUTTER_PULSE (and SET_FOCUS when the profile enables it); wait_for_image returns the first new .nef whose size is stable for 500 ms\n- [ ] Partial or temp files are ignored; timeout returns a clear error\n- [ ] A triplet via pulse + digiCamControl works on Windows (human reports)\n\n## Estimate\n1d (size M)\n\n## Dependencies\n- {{dep:H-08}} H-08 – Capture orchestrator, CameraSource, FakeCamera and safety\n- {{dep:R-04}} R-04 – Spike - digiCamControl on Windows\n\n## References\n- Spec: specification section 6.1\n- Milestone: M6 Guided RGB capture; area: Hardware ingest and capture; previous ID: GTP-083"
  },
